{"forms":[{"form":"affine transformations","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"An affine transformation does not in general preserve angles between lines or distances between points, but it does preserve ratios of distances between points lying on a straight line.","type":"example"},{"text":"Given an affine space X, every affine transformation on X can be represented as the composition of a linear transformation on X and a translation of X.","type":"example"},{"text":"Examples of affine transformations include translation, scaling, homothety, similarity, reflection, rotation, shear and compositions of them in any combination and sequence.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A geometric transformation that preserves lines and parallelism, but in general not lengths or angles; (more formally) an automorphism of an affine space: a mapping of an affine space onto itself that preserves both the dimension of any affine subspace and the ratio of the lengths of any pair of parallel line segments."],"topics":["geometry","linear-algebra","mathematics","sciences"]}],"synonyms":[{"sense":"geometric transformation that preserves lines and parallelism","word":"affinity"}],"upstream_line":442089,"upstream_offset":2091467066,"upstream_record_sha256":"bc4f44aa6181546efd1db7a20fde06097d2dee39fa23886d12ca463b33560d59","word":"affine transformation"}
